### Standards, specification and ownership

AuthorizedRoot now requires an explicit `AGENT_RUNTIME_DIR` rather than defaulting to `C:\RhaiQuality\runs`. It selects only a canonical Windows-separated path under the current user's `.local\share\agent-builds\rhai`, with exactly `<session>\run` beneath that prefix. Session length is 1–96, starts with ASCII alphanumeric, permits only ASCII alphanumeric/dot/underscore/hyphen thereafter, and rejects trailing dot; `run` is exactly lower-case. Missing values, another root, extra depth, traversal, slash aliases, UNC paths and noncanonical normalization are rejected. The overall root length guard reserves 80 characters within the existing 248-character path budget. Prefix matching includes the final separator and therefore cannot authorize a sibling project name by prefix alone.

This getter selects a path; it does not create, adopt, enumerate, delete or pin a directory. Real filesystem authority remains behind the unchanged `PinAuthorizedRoot → PinDirectory → BeginAllocation` path. Before opening directory handles, PinDirectory validates raw components, including dot/traversal, reserved DOS names and illegal Windows syntax; it requires an absolute fixed local drive. Its native pins reject reparse/non-directory components, retain ancestor identities and omit delete sharing. Allocation retains those pins and the existing exclusive creation/protected-object checks. Those unchanged mechanisms are source context here; their real native custody behavior is not established by this pure fixture.

MonitorAcceptanceDriver obtains its journal/evidence root through the same property. The retained legacy staging method's default also refers to it, but `ScopedRunner.Main` still dispatches only `--monitor` and the exact `--lease-client` form and refuses direct workload arguments. The unsafe legacy method remains unreachable from the public entry. No legacy route or shared root was enabled.

NativeScopePolicyFixture sets the environment only in its own owned child. It calls the actual production getter, checks the exact selected current-user path, tests nine missing/legacy/depth/traversal/trailing-dot/run-case/separator/UNC/session-length rejections, then checks the valid value again. It uses no file/directory creation or deletion API and never calls allocation or process launch. A getter exception or wrong result prints `NATIVE_PRIVATE_SCOPE_ASSERTION` and returns 1; complete success prints the exact PASS marker and returns 0. The claimed native length control is a 97-character session; the additional overall path-budget guard is source-reviewed, not separately exercised by that control.

BuildOnly now compiles this fixture with the nine actual production sources and runs that child before compiling the public runner, real driver and finite PayloadFixture. It still omits the broad synthetic fixture suite and does not launch the native driver in the compiler owner's job. The zero-argument repair omits Start-Process's optional ArgumentList only when the supplied argument-array count is zero. A nonempty array retains the existing quoted command line, cwd, redirects, exact Process handle acquisition and exit-code checks; a one-element empty argument is still represented by the quoted `""`, rather than being confused with no arguments.

The new compiler invocation uses the existing self-inclusive compiler job established before csc. The policy child has a 30-second limit, compilation a 180-second limit, and the overall compiler owner/watchdog and 4 MiB per-log caps remain in force. Adding the child does not change job/watchdog lifetime or failure disposition. Source review confirms the final BUILD_ONLY_PASS receipt is written only after owner-only accounting, successful exact-job disposition and watchdog callback drain; failure retains primary diagnostics then terminates the exact job with E0000003. The native observations below bind those paths for this slice.

### Concrete blockers

**[P2] Timeout and other observation failures discard original async driver logs.** At `public-success.ps1:40`, stdout/stderr are drained into ReadToEndAsync tasks, but saved only at line 50 after the normal process wait, status readback, both task waits and byte-cap checks. The explicit observer-timeout branch at lines 41–44 kills/waits the driver and throws before collecting either task. Lines 63–64 then manufacture an empty stdout file and place the observer exception in stderr, while disposing the process at line 65. Any real driver output produced before its timeout is lost. Unavailable exit status, a task-wait failure or the output-cap rejection likewise bypasses the original-output save. These placeholder files must not be exported under the appearance of original native driver logs. Preserve actual captured output on every terminal path, and record observer diagnostics separately. If a reader cannot complete, retain an explicit incomplete-capture observation rather than labelling an invented empty file as the original stream. The waits/drain and per-log bounds must remain finite.

**[P2] An exception after Start but before confirmed exit releases the observer's exact process owner and starts export.** The unconditional final `Process.Dispose()` does not terminate or wait for a running driver. For example, an exception acquiring/checking its handle, starting an async reader, or performing the first wait reaches that finally without confirmed exact child exit; the entry then inventories the runtime for export while the driver/monitor may still be changing it. The driver has its independent deadline, but the outer observer does not use that fact to prove its own terminal readback or settled evidence before exporting. Track whether Start actually succeeded and whether exact exit was confirmed; on exceptional started-child paths, perform bounded exact-process termination/wait and reader finalization before releasing the observer handle and claiming a terminal inventory. If that cannot be confirmed, preserve custody/session diagnostics and leave export/acceptance explicitly incomplete. This also makes `driver_launched` truthful: it is currently hard-coded true even if Start fails.

These are concrete branches in the proposed entry and violate the required failure evidence/retained-owner composition. They do not invalidate the accepted scope-policy/compiler slice and do not require rebuilding its unchanged binaries. No native run was attempted by this reviewer.

### Composition that is otherwise bound

The selected route is direct `MonitorAcceptanceDriver.exe`, with UseShellExecute=false, redirected pipes and the retained driver/runner sibling directory; it does not nest the driver in the compiler job or revive legacy `--source` launch. The driver source rejects ambient job membership before starting a client, establishes its own limited kill-on-close controller job, confirms client membership, and uses the public `ScopedRunner.exe --lease-client` transport. Its monitor is launched through the existing restricted-handle list/breakaway route and owns payload allocation/staging/job closure. These are reviewed source preconditions; actual uncontained native execution and successful allocation are still unobserved.

The entry hash-checks driver, runner, finite payload and existing compiler before reuse. It requires the exact new session `C:\Users\RhaiTest\.local\share\agent-builds\rhai\custody-success-20261008-b7f83433` to be absent, checks existing ancestors for reparses, and creates only owned `run` and sibling `input`. It hash-checks the copied `fixture.exe`. Only the driver's child environment receives the fresh `<session>\run` AGENT_RUNTIME_DIR; the parent environment is unchanged. The source directory is separate from the allocation parent/runtime, avoiding source/runtime overlap. The fixed fixture takes one relative record-file argument, writes `payload-started\r\n` under its runtime cwd, then exits 0; no Cargo/product executable is selected.

The argv matches the driver codec: `--mode success --source <owned input> --exe fixture.exe --expected-payload-exit 00000000 -- payload-record.txt`. The driver itself has an absolute 180-second watchdog, bounded launch-spec/frame/queue/journal parsing, client stderr drain, and exact final controller-job accounting. The outer entry has a 210-second observer wait, 10-second exact-exit wait after its timeout kill and bounded normal-path reader waits. Fixing exceptional finalization must preserve those finite controls and retain the exact Process while needed. The output tasks avoid wait-before-drain pipe deadlock in the normal path; the findings concern their exceptional custody/save path.

The requested success fields agree with the existing driver contract: client exit **78 / 0x0000004E**, payload exit 0, PayloadExited, confirmed cleanup/removal, and HostExported=false. The latter is the backend receipt's deliberate lack of a host-export acknowledgement, separate from this entry's later serial copy. Driver ValidateMode verifies those values against the unique new framed journal and evidence hashes, checks real runtime absence and returns 0 only through its terminal logic. The entry also freshly reads the preserved `runtime\payload-record.txt` snapshot, requires the exact CRLF content, one owned evidence directory and no residual `scoped-*` runtime directory. These are appropriate planned independent observations, not completed evidence.

The export framing retains the previous inventory/file/frame/chunk/serial/180-second bounds and retains guest evidence. After the observer custody fixes, its accepted BEGIN/END must bind actual terminal driver status, settled original streams, fresh host record and completed framed export; a failure must not use empty substitute logs or release an unconfirmed running observer child. Success alone will still not close the planned interruption, controlling-connection loss, unrelated sentinel, native Windows process, filesystem/TCP or product gates. ### Original stream custody blocker resolved

Lines36–46 and53–56 replace ReadToEndAsync with one **1024-character ReadAsync** task per redirected stream. Completed buffers are written and flushed immediately to their separate no-BOM UTF-8 original files before scheduling the next read. Each channel has its own 4 MiB cap; a cap violation retains the already written bounded original prefix, marks a channel error and rejects acceptance. EOF is obtained only from an actual zero-count reader completion. Task read errors are recorded rather than replaced with observer exception text in stderr.

The unified finally at lines74–89 drains completed actual buffers even after timeout, nonzero/unavailable status, handle/setup or reader failure. Its monotonic drain bound is ten seconds; EOF/error state is checked for **both** streams before pipes_drained can become true. Observer diagnostics live in observer.json.failure, separate from original driver stderr. If capture cannot be started or finished, a zero-length or partial captured file remains explicitly incomplete through pipes_drained=false/failure/retained custody, and cannot satisfy the success gate. File writers are flushed/closed before any serial inventory, making the captured original prefixes stable. An unfinished reader task and its stream handle are retained rather than disposed as though capture had completed.

Thus ordinary timeout/status/cap failures no longer discard complete asynchronous results or invent stderr under the appearance of original driver output. Actual native pipe behavior and these failure paths still require native evidence; this conclusion is the source readiness repair of the concrete prior blocker.

### Exact process owner blocker resolved

Start is now tracked truthfully and driver_launched is derived from it in observer.json, BEGIN and END. The exact retained Process object and Handle are used throughout; no PID lookup or foreign process selection is introduced. The independent 210-second observer loop continues incremental capture. Any exception after successful Start but before confirmed exit enters finally, checks that same Process, kills it if still running and waits at most ten seconds for exact exit. A termination/status exception is saved in failure and leaves the custody object retained.

The process and pipe readers are disposed only when exact_driver_exited and both original pipes_drained are true. If either is indeterminate, the Process/readers/pending tasks remain reachable in the entry's scope and retained_driver_handles is true. The guest session is never retired by the exporter. An observed exit followed by a status failure rejects acceptance while retaining the available status/log diagnostics; a failure before Start reports driver_launched=false, rather than pretending a child ran.

Success checks run only when started, exact exit, both EOF drains and an empty failure are all established. They then require driver exit0, the exact existing native client/payload/supervision/cleanup/removal fields, one owned evidence directory, a fresh independent payload record with exact CRLF bytes and absence of real scoped runtime directories. **Recursive runtime evidence inventory occurs only after that complete validated success.** Every observer/control failure exports only the stable captured stdout/stderr and observer.json and preserves runtime custody without enumerating changing runtime files. This resolves the prior premature disposal/inventory branch.

### Why the hypothesis follows from the current source

CreateRuntime constructs a protected DACL with precisely two full-control allow ACEs, current user and LocalSystem, both object/container inheritable, verifies it through the exact runtime handle and records runtime/parent identities. RemoveRuntimeCore separately requires this root's protected ACL and the exact allocation authorization and identities. RemoveRuntimeDirectoryContents also calls VerifyProtectedDacl for **every descendant**, requiring the protection bit and un-inherited exact flags (none for a file, OI|CI for a directory).

PayloadFixture's unchanged File.WriteAllText and the new focused fixture's ordinary Directory.CreateDirectory/File.WriteAllText calls specify no replacement child DACL. Microsoft's [ACE inheritance rules](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/secauthz/ace-inheritance-rules) say OI|CI parent ACEs are effective on both kinds of child, with directories retaining propagation. The [security descriptor control documentation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/secauthz/security-descriptor-control) distinguishes inherited entries from the protection bit, which blocks parent inheritance. Thus the protected private root can produce ordinary unprotected descendants with the same effective principals and permissions. The current per-child protection requirement rejects this legitimate shape even though authority has not widened. This is an inference from the source and documented inheritance, awaiting the frozen native assertion and actual ACL readback.

A safe implementation should keep VerifyProtectedDacl strict for the allocation root, staged creation/validation/executable pins, journals, saved evidence and pipes. A separate descendant-disposition verifier may accept the existing strict shape or the exact ordinary inherited shape: nonnull two-entry DACL; one current-user and one LocalSystem ordinary full-control allow ACE; effective inherited flags exactly Inherited for files or Inherited|OI|CI for directories; no extra or missing principal, denial, callback/conditional/object-specific ACE, opaque condition, permission mismatch, inherit-only/no-propagate/other unexpected ACE flags, or blanket allowance merely because AreAccessRulesProtected is false. The future code must bind this exception to runtime-descendant disposition, without changing staging/evidence/root verification. If immutable staged inputs must also stay protected at disposition, distinguish them with their staged identities; do not infer that classification from a mutable path alone. There is no persistent full staged-entry identity inventory in current RuntimeAllocation to support a new blanket classification claim.

The existing authority checks remain necessary independently of the ACL: exact held root/parent identity and usable 128-bit IDs; handle-based parent enumeration; no-follow child open; matching enumerated/opened ID and volume/type; duplicate-identity refusal; reparse/read-only/pending-disposition refusal; single hard link for files; bounded entries/depth/path/time; bottom-up held-directory empty/identity readback; durable REMOVE_INTENT/REMOVED grammar. OpenDispositionEntry uses READ_CONTROL on the same no-follow handle used for disposition and denies delete sharing. No inherited-ACL exception authorizes a different root, an unrecorded identity, recursive path-based deletion, an ACL rewrite, or a production fixture-token bypass. The separately typed production outcome/evidence receipt and shared finalization deadline remain unchanged. Extra-principal failure may retain a partially removed owned runtime; it must not claim atomic no-effect cleanup or a REMOVED receipt.

### Concrete finding — exact ordinary ACE validation is incomplete

**[P2] WindowsCustodyBackend.cs:1525–1528 — reject callback/opaque CommonAce entries.** Current VerifyPrivateDacl checks that each ACE is a CommonAce with AccessAllowed qualifier, exact flags, FullControl mask and one of the two required SIDs. A CommonAce can also be a callback ACE with those same qualifier/flags/mask/SID fields; the isCallback and opaque data are separate constructor parameters. Therefore two callback ACEs can satisfy all current predicates although they are not the ordinary full-control ACEs promised by this extension. The extra-principal WorldSid control cannot detect this case. Microsoft's [CommonAce constructor documentation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.security.accesscontrol.commonace.-ctor?view=net-10.0), also applicable to .NET Framework4.8.1, confirms this distinct callback/opaque representation; the [CommonAce class documentation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.security.accesscontrol.commonace?view=net-10.0) exposes IsCallback and OpaqueLength.

Reject common.IsCallback and any nonzero common.OpaqueLength (or require the exact ordinary AccessAllowed ACE type), in addition to the current predicates. No new SID, mask, flag, root or handle authority should be added. The protected branch inherited this gap from its previous implementation; the new inherited branch must not carry it forward under an exact-ordinary-ACE acceptance claim. This is a statically identified shape-validation defect, not an observed native exploit, race or permission-widening result. The two-entry SID/count, inheritance flag and access-mask checks otherwise match the proposed narrow contract. Rebind the corrected backend/hash/pin inventory and review only that correction before relying on the full GREEN source contract. No independent specialist race review is called for by this finding.

### Single exact-handle correction and ownership

Backend SHA **`aff42a19b8e05029be0293c413def0d5bd1858e4a0d04af7e2d48f4c4eb09ba1`** adds the DuplicateHandle declaration/helper and changes only capture's runtime source pin from a path reopen to DuplicatePinnedHandle(runtimeHandle). Native source and target are the current-process pseudohandle-1, desired access0 with DUPLICATE_SAME_ACCESS(2), inheritfalse, and no DUPLICATE_CLOSE_SOURCE. It duplicates only this already-authorized live allocation's SafeFileHandle within the allocation lock. No share mask, root/ancestor/path authorization, ACL or Job mechanism is relaxed. The original pin remains retained for allocation custody; the new independently owned SafeFileHandle is scoped to capture.

Microsoft's [DuplicateHandle documentation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/handleapi/nf-handleapi-duplicatehandle) supports that same-access duplication preserves rights, a false inherit flag makes the duplicate non-inheritable, and both handles reference the same object. This is an owned handle reference, not an independently reopened directory. The source does not use the close-source option or transfer the handle to another process. Its failure branch captures GetLastWin32Error before disposing a possible returned handle, preserves that error as Win32Exception, and rejects/disposes any null/invalid success handle. SafeFileHandle marshaling retains the source during the native call.

PinSingleDirectory owns only the duplicate, disposes it if construction/identity read fails, and normal using disposal closes only that duplicate. Original runtimeHandle is neither assigned nor closed by the helper. Capture immediately compares duplicate identity with RuntimeIdentity and retains existing plain-directory/tag, source identity/inventory/readback and finite finalization checks. Duplicates share object state, including file position: bounded ReadDirectoryEntries starts with RestartInfo on every enumeration, and capture/disposition are sequential under the same allocation lock, so this change introduces no competing root enumeration worker. No new unsafe native Job/handle race was found in this precise delta. Actual native compilation and corrected capture behavior remain required.

### Two concrete public-test contract corrections

1. **P2 — remove the prohibited spawn timeout.** `tests/sys_process_windows.rs:109` gives `spawn` `timeout: 5`. Existing public spawn rejects any timeout option (`unix.rs:2289–2295`); its handles select waits explicitly. Canonical Windows planning preserves shared feature-correct validation and ports the existing contract. This option would prevent the test from reaching the held-child/native-handle assertions once that contract is implemented. Remove the spawn timeout and retain the finite fixture/readiness bounds plus prerequisite outer native custody. No alternative deadline authority should be inferred from this option.

2. **P2 — use feature-correct timed-wait expressions.** Lines137 and152 use `cloned.wait(0)` and `child.wait(0)` in a target enabled for both float and no_float profiles. Existing Child registration exposes FLOAT seconds when floats are enabled and INT seconds only under no_float (`unix.rs:1911–1950`); the earlier saved regression also recorded the actual missing INT overload in a float profile. Use `0.0` for float-enabled profiles and `0` under no_float at both pending and cached timed waits. Otherwise a function-resolution failure replaces the intended pending/cached oracle. This applies to default/f32 float profiles as well as the explicit no_float variant.

These are source contract defects, not newly observed Windows REDs. Current `process.rs` still registers execution only under Unix and has no Windows backend module. The plan deliberately requires a native public registration/runtime RED before implementation; that future registration failure must be distinguished from the two avoidable fixture-context errors above. Native public-process execution readiness is not accepted at this checkpoint.

**P2 — failed handle closure can be followed by clean terminal publication.** In `native.rs`, Handle Drop at 59 calls CloseHandle but only writes stderr on failure. Port::finish at 268 removes its owning handle without returning that disposition. The final worker path at 1013 constructs a retired terminal result before dropping Owner, and then publishes that preconstructed result. Consequently a coded CloseHandle failure cannot become a Process cleanup diagnostic or change the clean result; the sole owning wrapper is also gone. This is a concrete failure-path/reporting gap, not an observed native close failure or a claim that valid ordinary handles routinely fail. Retire handles through checked normal-path closure before clean publication, with an explicit honest disposition when closure cannot be confirmed; keep RAII as exceptional fallback. Do not blindly retry an invalid or potentially reused numeric handle.

**P2 — failed activation acknowledges the public error before exact child closure is observed.** At 978, Owner::activate can fail after CreateProcessW has created the suspended child (assignment, identity recheck or resume). Its error branch sends the launch error through the channel at 989 immediately; only the following supervision loop drives exact termination, process wait, Job accounting and I/O closure. The receiver returns a plain io::Error and the caller does not receive the later retained Control report. Background ownership and its permit are retained, and there is no uncontained resume fallback, but the public failed-spawn result is not bound to completion or honest bounded-incomplete cleanup. The canonical plan lines 790–793 requires failed creation/assignment/resume/pipe setup to terminate/wait the owned suspended child and retire the reservation. Defer the failed-activation acknowledgement until bounded disposition, or return the corresponding incomplete Process report while preserving the background owner when closure cannot be confirmed.

The remaining inspected source has a coherent ownership route: one worker owns the exact process/thread/Job and pipe endpoints independently of client leases; clone/cache use one immutable Control result; only the explicit standard handles are inherited; Managed assignment and membership checking precede resume. Boxed OVERLAPPED structures and pipe buffers remain alive through the observed completion of each pending operation, including exceptional cancellation. The worker bounds output capture, diagnostics and cleanup publication, retains custody after incomplete publication, and does not overwrite an existing primary cause. This is source reasoning, not native Job/cancellation/grandchild acceptance.

The latest draft corrections were read directly: cancelling an already-ended port preserves its observed EOF; a completed read reporting native broken pipe/no-data/EOF remains complete even if cancellation raced it, while abort or cancelled ordinary data remains incomplete. Output and stdin pumping are followed by exact process/Job observation, and fully observed exit + Job0 + I/O closure now returns before committing a new deadline. Observed I/O/wait/accounting errors therefore precede a newly observed Timeout, while an earlier primary cause remains unchanged. ### Windows Rust source correction review — checked retirement and failed-start acknowledgement

Source-only continuation of the same combined review on LLLM. The loaded governing Matt-Pocock/Wayfinder revision is unchanged `f3fc5632f401156837ee3872f14fe33ccf1024ea`; project/canonical technical and ownership rules remain those recorded above. The retired global harness is disabled and unread. Owner autonomous authorization and the LLLM-only boundary remain in force. No process/build/test, guest/config/Git/source changes, cleanup or additional agent work was performed. Only this report is appended; its entire previous 194410-byte prefix (`d2e17391814068039bfb33c9ab2b2cd3e0f69385b19a38d42dcc4ec19e7c8a5d`) is preserved.

The two prior P2 findings are corrected in the inspected source:

- **Checked retirement:** Handle::try_close disarms on successful CloseHandle and caches a refused disposition without retrying its numerical value (native.rs:66–84); exceptional Drop does not retry a cached refusal. Port::finish retains its wrapper and close error on refusal (297–319), and the worker collects those errors before exit/deadline decisions (879–892, 967). Normal Owner retirement checks input/output endpoints and their events, parent stdio duplicates, thread, process, Job, cwd pins/capability and finally wake (893–929). A partial refusal records incomplete cleanup, preserves the existing cause, publishes an immutable diagnostic report and quarantines the exact owner/permit; the worker parks without using the partially retired handles. A clean terminal is built and published only after successful checked retirement and disposal of the disarmed Owner and Permit (1182–1189). Cwd now transfers its unique cap_std File handle into that same checked native owner, avoiding unchecked File Drop on normal retirement (cwd.rs:74–78, 121–127).
- **Failed-start acknowledgement:** StartFailure distinguishes BeforeCreate I/O from AfterCreate(Terminal). The activation result and unwind are recorded without immediately acknowledging the failed launch (1116–1139). Only bounded incomplete publication sends AfterCreate while retaining background custody (1161–1169); actual successful retirement first drops Owner and Permit, publishes the same terminal clone, then sends AfterCreate (1182–1189). windows.rs:617–624 converts this into the typed Process cause/report. The success launch acknowledgement remains separate. This now gives the failed-start caller the cleanup disposition that the prior plain early I/O error omitted.

Wake ownership is serialized by Mutex<Option<Handle>>. API cancellation locks it while signaling; normal retirement locks it while closing and removing the owner. Only the worker can close it. The raw handle borrowed for worker waits cannot race an API-side close, and the wake close occurs last. Successfully closed owners are disarmed; refused owners are parked without numerical retries. No new race or partial-retirement use was identified in these corrected paths.

**P2 remaining completion-proof gap — normal paths bypass the new exceptional retention rule.** Port Drop now correctly treats ERROR_IO_INCOMPLETE, ERROR_INVALID_HANDLE and ERROR_INVALID_PARAMETER from GetOverlappedResult as insufficient proof of completion and retains its stack/OVERLAPPED/buffer (321–348). However Port::connect still clears pending for every error except IO_INCOMPLETE (167–173), and Port::poll clears pending in its catch-all error arm (264–266), including INVALID_HANDLE/PARAMETER. On such a result, normal cancellation/retirement can therefore treat the operation as no longer pending and ultimately free its allocations without a completion observation; the protective Drop path is bypassed. In particular INVALID_PARAMETER does not itself prevent a subsequent CloseHandle from succeeding, so checked handle closure alone does not supply the missing completion proof. Retain the pending/custody state for the same indeterminate errors in connect/poll, and bind failure publication to an honest retained disposition rather than treating an API lookup failure as an I/O completion. This is a source failure-contract finding; no native occurrence, memory corruption or ordinary valid-handle failure was observed. The report does not infer that this error is routinely reachable with the correctly owned inputs.

Scoped disposition: the original failed-close clean-publication and premature failed-start acknowledgement findings are closed by source inspection for the snapshot below. The normal completion-proof gap remains open. The corrected source has not yet been compiled or executed in the retained initial cross-check rows; those earlier rows keep their original source bindings. Native Windows product process/Job/cwd/pipe behavior and release/resource gates remain unaccepted. Previously accepted native compiler/custody tiny-fixture proofs are unchanged.

Normal checked CloseHandle records close_attempted before the native call; refusal fails the observation and Drop does not retry a potentially reused value or claim closure (269–288). An exceptional close before any normal attempt remains a single RAII fallback. The native observer requests only query-limited/synchronize access, compares creation FILETIME before accepting PID attribution, reads the image from the held exact handle, waits finitely, checks signalled exit and checks normal close. New HeldFixture observer ownership is joined on both readback and Drop, and release is published on Drop; no observer thread is deliberately detached. The child writes JSON before its atomic ready record, embeds native PID/creation time, waits at most ten seconds for release and emits only the fixed small output. The observer readiness and native-exit bounds are three and five seconds; public run is bounded by the host five-second default. Successful assertions require both the independent native observation and public result. A failed/panicking observer join is not successful native child-closure proof: Drop's best-effort release/join and the existing TempDir removal do not promote a failed test to accepted cleanup. Future native execution remains subject to the planned exact outer custody and failure readback.

The argv/environment test explicitly separates literal test argv with `--`, compares all reconstructed child strings from fresh JSON, verifies case-insensitive PATH removal, remove-then-override of the echo key, env_clear true/false behavior and an unchanged parent environment. **P2 — missing local observer is still treated as permission to retire a possibly live fixture root.** PendingFixture::observe reads readiness and opens/checks a native handle before assigning self.observer (660–664). If readiness times out, record parsing fails or OpenProcess/identity checking fails after spawn, Drop has no stored observer. Track a unified explicit confirmed-closure state (set only after exact exit and checked close), and preserve/log the owned root when that state is unconfirmed, including failures before local observer installation. Successfully finished local fixtures must separately mark confirmation so their roots can retire normally.

ThrowObserver joins on success/unwind before PendingFixture drops. PendingFixture acquires the flag and retains/logs the exact root when it remains false (690–694). The existing action flag only records a successful Kill call; it does not change the supplied native exit. Consequently the retained driver's negative mode validation rejects the actual intended kill, before the entry can accept or perform its fresh payload readback.
